Hi. Thanks. Yeah, so, um... I guess what finally made me make an appointment is that I just keep turning in these applications and research notes with really small, but super embarrassing mistakes. Like, recently I mislabeled an artwork date. It was for this Renaissance art piece, which is actually a really fascinating era—like, the way the patron system worked back then is wild, especially if you look at the lesser-known families outside the Medicis. I actually went down this huge rabbit hole reading about their letters to artists last week instead of sleeping, it's just super interesting. 

But, um, anyway, yeah... I'll put the wrong date, or I'll just completely skip a question on an application form. And the frustrating part is I honestly thought I had checked them. I'll sit there at the library or this coffee shop I go to—they have this amazing cold brew, by the way, they roast their own beans—and I'll look over everything, but I still somehow just miss these obvious things.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, no, it's definitely not a new thing. I mean, even way back in elementary school, my teachers would always write on my report cards or tell my parents that I had really good ideas, but I just kept forgetting to actually turn in my worksheets. 

I remember this one worksheet in fourth grade, it was about the solar system. Which, honestly, space is terrifying if you think about it too much. Like, the sheer scale of the universe? We watched this documentary once in science class, and it just blew my mind how small Earth is compared to literally everything else. I actually still watch a lot of astrophysics videos on YouTube when I'm supposed to be doing other things. The algorithm just knows exactly what to recommend, you know? Like it'll show me a video on black holes and suddenly three hours have passed. 

But, um... wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, school. Yeah, I'd lose the instructions for projects all the time, or just miss these really little details on assignments. It got to the point where my parents literally had to check my backpack every single night. I had this really cool Spider-Man backpack, actually, the kind where the eyes were reflective? But yeah, if they didn't physically dig through it and pull the crumpled papers out from the bottom, nothing would ever make it back to the teacher.

No, not really. I mean, I'm pretty chill physically. I can sit in a chair for hours, especially if I'm reading. I used to read so much when I was a kid. Yeah, it's definitely causing actual problems now. It's not just an occasional frustration anymore. Like, my mentor actually sat me down recently to talk about it. Dr. Harris, he's this really brilliant guy, he wears these amazing vintage tweed jackets. I'm actually pretty sure he gets them from this specific thrift store downtown. Have you ever been to that one on 4th Street? It's huge, they have a whole basement section just for vinyl records, which is so rare to find now. I spent like three hours down there last week looking for old jazz albums because I just got a record player for my birthday. The sound quality on vinyl really is just warmer, you know? People think it's just a hipster thing, but the analog audio actually captures frequencies that digital compresses out. 

But, um... wait, what was I saying about Dr. Harris? Oh, right. Yeah, so he told me that my lack of follow-through is starting to really hurt opportunities that I should be ready for by now. Because of all this, I’ve been submitting my work late, and I actually completely missed a couple of application deadlines recently. Which really sucks because one of the programs was in Chicago, and I’ve always wanted to live there. The architecture in Chicago is supposed to be incredible, especially the historical Art Deco buildings. They do those boat tours on the river where they explain all the history of the skyline, which sounds so cool... but yeah, I missed the deadline because I just lost track of the days, so I guess I'm not doing that.

No, I wouldn't say I've been feeling sad or anxious. I mean, I get a little stressed sometimes when I realize I've messed up a deadline, but it's not like a persistent worry or anything. My teacher, Mrs. Gable, always had these hard peppermint candies in a glass bowl on her piano, and they were always super dusty. I never wanted to eat one, but she'd always offer.

Like, I'd go to the library and check out the maximum number of books they'd let you take, which I think was like fifteen? And I'd just sit in this one beanbag chair in my room and read all weekend. It was this bright green beanbag, super comfortable until the little foam beads started leaking out. I tried to tape it up with duct tape, but then the tape got all sticky and gross, so my mom eventually threw it away. I was actually really mad about that for a while. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really jump up or run around or anything. And I don't think I'm impulsive. I'm actually pretty quiet in groups. I usually just listen to people talk. I have this one friend, Dave, who literally never stops talking. He's always interrupting people, and it's kind of annoying, honestly. Like, we'll be in the middle of a conversation about a movie—we just saw that new sci-fi one, the one with the really good CGI? The way they rendered the alien landscapes was incredible, I actually looked up the visual effects studio that did it, they're based in New Zealand, which seems like a really cool place to live. The scenery there is gorgeous. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right, fidgeting. Yeah, no, I don't really do that. I just sit there. Sometimes I'll tap my foot if I'm listening to music, but that's just keeping time, you know? I play a little guitar, mostly acoustic stuff. I tried to learn electric, but the amp was too loud for my apartment. But yeah, no, I don't really have the hyper or impulsive stuff. Just the spacing out.\n\nDoctor: Would you say these attention slips have caused real problems for you—like affecting your job performance, relationships, or finances—or are they more of an occasional frustration?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely causing actual problems now. It's not just an occasional frustration anymore. Like, my mentor actually sat me down recently to talk about it. Dr. Harris, he's this really brilliant guy, he wears these amazing vintage tweed jackets. I'm actually pretty sure he gets them from this specific thrift store downtown. Have you ever been to that one on 4th Street? It's huge, they have a whole basement section just for vinyl records, which is so rare to find now. I spent like three hours down there last week looking for old jazz albums because I just got a record player for my birthday. The sound quality on vinyl really is just warmer, you know? People think it's just a hipster thing, but the analog audio actually captures frequencies that digital compresses out. \n\nBut, um... wait, what was I saying about Dr. Harris? Oh, right. Yeah, so he told me that my lack of follow-through is starting to really hurt opportunities that I should be ready for by now. Because of all this, I’ve been submitting my work late, and I actually completely missed a couple of application deadlines recently. Which really sucks because one of the programs was in Chicago, and I’ve always wanted to live there. The architecture in Chicago is supposed to be incredible, especially the historical Art Deco buildings. They do those boat tours on the river where they explain all the history of the skyline, which sounds so cool... but yeah, I missed the deadline because I just lost track of the days, so I guess I'm not doing that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed periods where you feel unusually energetic or elated, need much less sleep than usual, or find your thoughts racing much faster than normal?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I really get like that. Like, unusually energetic? Not really. I mean, I like my sleep. I usually need a solid eight hours, or I'm just totally useless the next day. Sometimes I'll stay up late if I'm really into a video game or something. I've been playing this huge open-world RPG lately, and it's so easy to just lose track of time because there are so many side quests. Like, you start off just trying to deliver a letter to some village, and then suddenly you're fighting a dragon on top of a mountain, and it's 3 AM. The graphics are amazing, though. The way the light reflects off the water when the sun rises in the game? It's honestly beautiful. I read that the developers spent like two years just perfecting the water physics. Which is kind of crazy when you think about it, spending two years of your life just coding how water ripples. I wonder what that office environment is like. Probably a lot of energy drinks and late nights. \n\nBut, um... wait, what did you ask? Racing thoughts? I mean, my mind wanders a lot, obviously, like I'm always thinking about random stuff, but it doesn't feel like it's going super fast or out of control. And I don't get super elated or anything. I'm pretty even-keeled, mood-wise. Just... easily distracted, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling persistently sad, anxious, or worried lately, or noticed a loss of interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been feeling sad or anxious. I mean, I get a little stressed sometimes when I realize I've messed up a deadline, but it's not like a persistent worry or anything. And I definitely still like doing the things I enjoy. Like, I was just talking about my record player, right? I've been spending so much time listening to those jazz albums. I even started reading up on the history of jazz in the 1920s. Did you know that a lot of the early jazz musicians didn't even know how to read sheet music? They just played entirely by ear and improvised everything. It's wild to think about that level of raw talent. I tried to learn piano when I was a kid, but I hated practicing scales. My teacher, Mrs. Gable, always had these hard peppermint candies in a glass bowl on her piano, and they were always super dusty. I never wanted to eat one, but she'd always offer. \n\nAnyway, what was the question? Oh, losing interest in things. Yeah, no, I still have plenty of interests. If anything, I have too many interests, I just bounce between them. But I'm not depressed or anxious or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, I don't really know if it affects my social life or home stuff as much. I mean, my apartment is a bit messy right now, but my roommate is honestly way worse. He leaves his dishes in the sink for days. Like, he made this huge pot of chili on Tuesday and the pot is literally still sitting there soaking. Which is crazy because chili is so easy to just put in Tupperware and freeze. I actually love making chili, but I use ground turkey instead of beef. It's leaner, you know? And if you add enough cumin and smoked paprika, you can't even tell the difference. Spices are so expensive though. I went to the grocery store to buy saffron once for a paella recipe I saw on YouTube, and it was like twenty dollars for a tiny little jar. The guy in the video made it look so easy, cooking it outside over this big open fire in Spain. I'd love to go to Spain someday. They take a siesta in the middle of the day, which honestly makes so much sense for human productivity. 

But, uh... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, home and relationships. Um, I'm not really sure. I guess I forget to do my laundry sometimes, or I'll kind of zone out when my friends are talking if it's a really long story, but it doesn't feel like a huge deal. It's mostly just my school work and the mentor stuff where I really notice it messing things up.

Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.  Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety. Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others. Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world.  Subtypes: Negative Self-Beliefs, Negative Beliefs about Others, Negative Worldview\n[S073] Distorted_Cognitions_With_Blame: Persistent, distorted cognitions about the cause or consequences of the traumatic event that lead the individual to blame himself/herself or others. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nNo, not really. I mean, I'm pretty chill physically. I can sit in a chair for hours, especially if I'm reading. I used to read so much when I was a kid. Like, I'd go to the library and check out the maximum number of books they'd let you take, which I think was like fifteen? And I'd just sit in this one beanbag chair in my room and read all weekend. It was this bright green beanbag, super comfortable until the little foam beads started leaking out. I tried to tape it up with duct tape, but then the tape got all sticky and gross, so my mom eventually threw it away. I was actually really mad about that for a while. \n\nBut yeah, I don't really jump up or run around or anything. And I don't think I'm impulsive. I'm actually pretty quiet in groups. I usually just listen to people talk. I have this one friend, Dave, who literally never stops talking. He's always interrupting people, and it's kind of annoying, honestly. Like, we'll be in the middle of a conversation about a movie—we just saw that new sci-fi one, the one with the really good CGI? The way they rendered the alien landscapes was incredible, I actually looked up the visual effects studio that did it, they're based in New Zealand, which seems like a really cool place to live. The scenery there is gorgeous. \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right, fidgeting. Yeah, no, I don't really do that. I just sit there. Sometimes I'll tap my foot if I'm listening to music, but that's just keeping time, you know? I play a little guitar, mostly acoustic stuff. I tried to learn electric, but the amp was too loud for my apartment. But yeah, no, I don't really have the hyper or impulsive stuff. Just the spacing out.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"

Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event. Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences.
